# Effect review — every candidate effect, three variants each

**This is not a feature design.** It is a content-selection round. Every question is one
candidate effect, and every answer is a ruling on whether that effect enters the game and in
which form. Nothing here specifies how anything is built.

**1,067 questions.** There is no branching: every question is independent, so the count you see is
the count you answer. Rejecting is one keystroke.

## 0. How to review this document

**Every question is one effect.** The three lettered options are three versions of the *same*
effect — sometimes three strengths, sometimes three mechanisms, sometimes three scopes,
whichever fork was worth putting to you for that particular idea. Pick the one you want.

- **(a) (b) (c)** — the three versions. Pick one and that version enters the CSV as approved.
- **(d) reject** — the effect does not enter the game in any form.
- **Write your own** — the free-text box is on every question, always. If none of the three is
  right, type the version you want; your words go into the CSV verbatim as the approved effect,
  not a paraphrase of them.

**One option is always marked as the recommendation, and it is never (d).** Enter presses whatever
is marked, so holding Enter approves rather than rejects. The mark moves to *use what I wrote* the
moment you type, so Enter can never discard what you wrote. Accepting the recommendation without
moving the mark is recorded as its own state — being shown what Enter does is not the same as
choosing it.

**Answers are revisitable.** Go back to any earlier question and change it; nothing is lost.

### What each question header tells you

```
**Name** — slot, class code, provenance. One line saying what the effect is.
```

- **slot** — where the effect lives on a card: `suit`, `rank`, `type`, `stamp`, `skill`,
  `consumable`, `rule`, or `status`. Every effect here fits one of them; that was the filter for
  getting in at all.
- **class code** — its cell in the design-space taxonomy (`C4`, `H6`, `P3`…). Two effects sharing
  a class code are competing for the same design space, which is why they are next to each other.
- **provenance** — the document or wiki it was mined from, or `generated` if it was written to
  fill a class the corpus left empty.

### The order

Grouped by family, then by class within the family. **An upgrade sits directly after the effect
it upgrades** — 11 questions are positioned that way, so when you see a stronger or less
restricted version of the question you just answered, you are being asked to price the pair.

---

## 1. What was mined, and what happened to it

**2,234 candidate effects** were extracted from every text document in the repo and from the
Balatro and Cryptid reference wikis:

| Source | Extracted |
|---|---|
| `CARD_CATALOG.csv` | 378 |
| `DESIGN_DOC.md`, `DESIGN_RECOMMENDATIONS.md`, `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md` | 758 |
| `gam draft.txt`, `random potential effects.csv`, `curated effects pre grid.csv`, `todo.md`, `pokerpatience.txt` | 459 |
| balatrowiki.org — jokers, modifiers, stakes, blinds, tags, challenges | 249 |
| Cryptid and Pokermon mod wikis | 390 |

148 were folded as exact restatements, leaving 2,086 to judge. **1,160 were dropped:**

| Reason | Dropped |
|---|---|
| `DUPLICATE` — the same design space as an effect already asked about | 747 |
| `NO_MECHANIC` — art direction, naming, engineering, or a taxonomy heading | 130 |
| `RESKIN` — a numeric or suit reskin of a more general effect | 119 |
| `ALREADY_A_RULE` — restates a rule the game already has | 113 |
| `NOT_APPLICABLE` — needs a held hand, discards per round, or blinds | 51 |

**926 survived and were rewritten as grid-native mechanics** — foreign vocabulary translated, and
reach expressed in this game's four-dimensional coordinate wherever the effect could carry it.
**131 more were written from scratch**: 69 to fill taxonomy classes the corpus left empty, 62
for eighteen classes the taxonomy itself had failed to name — progress-relative scoring, placement
order, deck-composition reads, slot topology, in-run quests, and the whole of family X below.

**Ten more were recovered by re-reading `gam draft.txt` by hand.** The mining pass skipped that
file's art-direction and circus-history sections wholesale, and mechanics had been written
parenthetically inside them. Those ten carry their braindump line number in the question text.

Every drop is recorded with its reason and, for duplicates, the id of the effect it was folded
into — so any category can be resurfaced if you disagree with a call.

## 2. The design space this covers

The taxonomy behind the class codes is **24 families, 226 classes**, crossed with six structural
axes (slot, trigger, scope, duration, valence, agency). **216 of the 226 classes are represented
here.** The 10 that are not are exactly family N — view, camera and UI — **excluded by your
ruling**; the taxonomy still carries them so the hole stays visible.

The taxonomy was audited once the corpus pass was finished, and it had a bias worth naming: built
from the engine's hook surface and from Balatro, it described **effects that fire and add**, and
had almost no vocabulary for effects that *read state* or that key off *absence*. Eighteen classes
were added to correct it, including a new **family X — absence, failure and the unplayed**, which is
the mirror of every other family in the document.

---


## Family A - Scoring — the number itself


### A1 - Flat point add

- **Q0001** `[root]` — **Cheering Team card** — skill, A1, from random potential effects.csv:5. Pays its own suit · **(a)** Each scored card sharing this card's suit gains extra points · **(b)** Each scored card sharing this card's suit gains extra points, scaling with how many of them there are · **(c)** Each scored card sharing this card's suit gains extra points, and this card gains the same again · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q0002** `[root]` — **Crowd glow** — rule, A1, from DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §F3 Music, Concerts & Festivals. The crowd trickles in regardless · **(a)** On a low-scoring turn, a small amount of fame arrives anyway · **(b)** On a turn that scores nothing at all, a small amount of fame arrives anyway · **(c)** On a low-scoring turn, fame arrives inversely to what you scored, so the worst turns pay most · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q0003** `[root]` — **Extra Point** — skill, A1, from CARD_CATALOG. Gain extra flat points on score (+10) · **(a)** +10 points when this card scores · **(b)** +2 points for each card in the line this card scores in · **(c)** +10 points when this card scores, doubling each further time it scores in the same show · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q0004** `[root]` — **The Pose (Sandow)** — skill, A1, from DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §B3 Strength & Endurance. Scores by standing still · **(a)** Scores by being spotlit and doing nothing, rather than by melding · **(b)** Scores by being spotlit and doing nothing, paying more the longer it holds the pose · **(c)** Scores by being spotlit and doing nothing, and cannot score by melding at all · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q0005** `[root]` — **The Sparkler** — skill, A1, from CARD_CATALOG. Straight into the column score · **(a)** When this card scores, +10 flat points go to its column's score · **(b)** When this card scores, flat points equal to its rank go to its column's score · **(c)** When this card scores, +10 flat points go to whichever of its lines scored lowest · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)

### A2 - Additive mult

- **Q0006** `[root]` — **Bootstraps** — skill, A2, from balatro. Paid by what is in your pocket · **(a)** Gains a multiplier for every fixed amount of gold you hold · **(b)** Gains a multiplier for every fixed amount of gold, and spending gold mid-show lowers it immediately · **(c)** Gains a multiplier for gold held, capped, and converts overflow gold into flat points · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0007** `[root]` — **Combo card** — skill, A2, from random potential effects.csv:6. Scored cards keep the hand's value · **(a)** During scoring, each card gains temporary points equal to the hand it scored in · **(b)** During scoring, each card gains permanent points equal to the hand it scored in, once · **(c)** During scoring, each card gains temporary points equal to the hand it scored in, doubled if it scored in two lines · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0008** `[root]` — **Diamond type** — type, A2, from curated effects pre grid.csv:92. Twice the score, twice the price · **(a)** Doubles this card's score, and costs 3 more when traded at shops · **(b)** Doubles this card's score, and it cannot be sold at all · **(c)** Doubles this card's score, and every other card of its suit costs more at shops · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q0009** `[root]` — **More Poker card** — skill, A2, from random potential effects.csv:3. Poker hands pay double · **(a)** Poker hands score double · **(b)** Poker hands score double, but only the highest hand of each act · **(c)** Poker hands score double, and non-poker melds score nothing · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q0010** `[root]` — **Noble Family card** — skill, A2, from random potential effects.csv:7. Face cards feed each other · **(a)** Each scored face card raises the points of the next scored face card by 1 · **(b)** Each scored face card raises the next one by 1, and the chain resets at the end of each act · **(c)** Each scored face card raises the next by 1, with no reset, so the chain runs the whole show · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0011** `[root]` — **Per-act score multiplier** — rule, A2, from DESIGN_DOC.md §2 Core Game Loop refinements. Later acts are worth more · **(a)** Each act carries a rising score multiplier, so the final act is worth the most · **(b)** Only the final act carries an extra multiplier · **(c)** Each act's multiplier rises, and skipping an act carries its multiplier into the next · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)

- **Q0016** `[root]` — **Swashbuckler** — skill, A3, from balatro. Paid by what your collection is worth · **(a)** Adds the total sell value of your other cards to the multiplier · **(b)** Adds the total sell value of your other cards to the multiplier, and selling any of them lowers it at once · **(c)** Adds the sell value of your single most valuable other card, doubled · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q0017** `[root]` — **The Advance Campaign** — skill, A3, from CARD_CATALOG. ### A4 - Higher operators

- **Q0020** `[root]` — **Abstract Card** — skill, A4, from cryptid. Raises the score to a power · **(a)** Raises the act's multiplier to a power rather than adding or multiplying, with a chance of being destroyed each show · **(b)** Raises the act's multiplier to a power, with no destruction risk but a very small exponent · **(c)** Raises the multiplier to a power that grows the longer this card survives, with a rising chance of destruction · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0021** `[root]` — **Critical Deck** — rule, A4, from cryptid. A coin flip on the exponent · **(a)** Deck: after each act, a small chance the multiplier is squared, and a smaller chance it is square-rooted · **(b)** Deck: after each act, a small chance the multiplier is squared, with no downside · **(c)** Deck: after each act, the multiplier is raised to a random power between a half and two · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q0022** `[root]` — **Googol Play Card** — skill, A4, from cryptid. A tiny chance at an absurd number · **(a)** A small chance per act of an enormous multiplier, and nothing otherwise · **(b)** A small chance per act of an enormous multiplier, with a modest one the rest of the time · **(c)** A small chance of an enormous multiplier, and the chance improves each act it fails · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)

### A5 - Retrigger

- **Q0023** `[root]` — **Double Trigger** — stamp, A5, from CARD_CATALOG. All of this card's effects trigger twice, once per scoring pass · **(a)** All of this card's effects trigger twice · **(b)** All of this card's effects trigger twice, but only on the first line it completes each show · **(c)** This card's effects trigger once more for each line it belongs to, so a card scoring in a row and a column fires three times · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0024** `[root]` — **Echoing Trigger** — skill, A5, from CARD_CATALOG. All triggers on the grid repeat once, per card, per scoring pass · **(a)** Every card's triggers on the grid repeat once per scoring pass · **(b)** Every card's triggers in this card's own row and column repeat once · **(c)** Every card's triggers on the grid repeat once, and this card is destroyed at the end of the show · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q0025** `[root]` — **Exposed** — skill, A5, from cryptid. Retriggers the plain ones, kills the faces · **(a)** Retriggers every scoring non-face card twice more, and debuffs every face card · **(b)** Retriggers every scoring non-face card twice more, and face cards simply score normally · **(c)** Retriggers whichever card type is rarer on the board, and debuffs the commoner one · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0026** `[root]` — **Hanging Chad** — skill, A5, from balatro. Re-fires whichever card went first · **(a)** Retriggers the first card of a scoring line two extra times · **(b)** Retriggers the first and last card of a scoring line one extra time each · **(c)** Retriggers the first card of every scoring line once, across every line that scores · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0027** `[root]` — **Seltzer** — skill, A5, from balatro. Retriggers everything, then burns out · **(a)** Retriggers every scoring card for the next ten acts, then is destroyed · **(b)** Retriggers every scoring card for the next three acts, then is destroyed · **(c)** Retriggers every scoring card until it has retriggered a fixed total number of times, then is destroyed · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0028** `[root]` — **The Aerialist** — skill, A5, from CARD_CATALOG. Once per submit, re-scores the topmost meld, but is destroyed permanently if her column ever fails to score · **(a)** Once per submit, re-scores the highest meld; destroyed for good if her column ever fails to score · **(b)** Once per submit, re-scores the highest meld; destroyed for good the first time her column fails to score twice running · **(c)** Once per submit, re-scores any meld you choose; each use carries a 1-in-8 chance of destroying her · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0029** `[root]` — **The Clock Hang** — skill, A5, from CARD_CATALOG. May be placed half-off the grid past the last column; while hanging it is double-spotlit (its skill fires twice), but any board-shift effect discards it · **(a)** May be placed half off the grid past the last column; while hanging its skill fires twice, and any board-shift effect discards it · **(b)** May be placed half off the grid past any edge; while hanging its skill fires twice and it cannot be part of any meld · **(c)** May be placed half off the grid; while hanging it fires twice, and it falls off at the end of the act unless a line scored · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q0030** `[root]` — **The Drone Swarm** — skill, A5, from CARD_CATALOG. - **Q0031** `[root]` — **The Encore** — stamp, A5, from CARD_CATALOG. If this card's skill triggered this show, there is a seeded 50% chance it triggers once more at show end · **(a)** If this card's skill triggered this show, a 50% chance it triggers once more at show end · **(b)** If this card's skill triggered this show, it triggers once more at show end, guaranteed · **(c)** If this card's skill triggered this show, a 50% chance per trigger that it fires again at show end, capped at three · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0032** `[root]` — **The Fanchant** — skill, A5, from CARD_CATALOG. ### A9 - Deferred payout

- **Q0048** `[root]` — **Grock's Instrument** — skill, A9, from CARD_CATALOG. Each act this card would score, it instead hops one column away and banks the points; when it has no legal hop left, it scores everything banked at once · **(a)** Each act it would score, it hops one column instead and banks the points; when it cannot hop, it pays out the whole bank · **(b)** Each act it would score, it banks the points instead; cue at any time to pay the bank out · **(c)** Each act it would score, it hops one column and banks double; if another effect ever moves it, the bank is lost · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q0049** `[root]` — **Mid-show instant tips** — rule, A9, from DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §A13 Korea & Southeast Asia. - **Q0079** `[root]` — **Clusterfuck/Cluster** — rule, B1, from cryptid. - **Q0808** `[root]` — **The Net** — skill, Q6, from CARD_CATALOG. A prop that leaves its grid · **(a)** Props this card spawns cross into the next grid at the same row and continue · **(b)** Props this card spawns cross into the next grid and continue at full strength through every grid · **(c)** Props this card spawns cross into the next grid, halving in strength at each boundary · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)

- **Q0814** `[root]` — **The Costume Change** — rule, Q8, from generated. - **Q0815** `[root]` — **The Handoff** — skill, Q8, from generated. Consumable: reveals all fog-of-war nodes within 2 edges of the player's position, including contents · **(a)** Consumable: reveals every fogged map node within two steps, contents included · **(b)** Consumable: reveals every fogged map node on the whole route, but only their types, not their contents · **(c)** Consumable: reveals every fogged node within two steps and lets you swap two of them · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q0865** `[root]` — **The Mitt Camp Reading** — consumable, S1, from CARD_CATALOG. - **Q0866** `[root]` — **The Panorama** — consumable, S1, from CARD_CATALOG. ### S2 - Route manipulation

- **Q0867** `[root]` — **Branching escalating-difficulty paths** — rule, S2, from gam draft.txt:721. Branching paths of rising difficulty · **(a)** Map: branching paths of escalating difficulty, splitting roguelike routes from preset puzzle levels · **(b)** Map: branching paths of escalating difficulty, where clearing one path unlocks a shortcut on another · **(c)** Map: branching paths where each path has its own theme and its own escalating difficulty curve · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q0868** `[root]` — **Calendar shows (Inti Raymi)** — rule, S2, from DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §A11 South America. Festivals on a fixed calendar · **(a)** Bonus festivals appear at fixed intervals along the route, every Nth node · **(b)** Bonus festivals appear at fixed intervals, and reaching one early carries the bonus forward · **(c)** Bonus festivals appear at fixed intervals, and you may spend fame to move one closer · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q0869** `[root]` — **FTL constellation map** — rule, S2, from DESIGN_DOC.md §15 Map v4 (FTL, latest). A constellation you cannot walk back · **(a)** Map: a constellation of cities with no backtracking; touring builds fame, and hazards and weather occur between them · **(b)** Map: a constellation with no backtracking, and each city visited raises the odds of hazards on the next leg · **(c)** Map: a constellation with no backtracking, and you plan the whole route before the tour begins · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0870** `[root]` — **Map alternative topologies** — rule, S2, from DESIGN_DOC.md §15 Map v4 (FTL, latest). The route has no end, only a shape · **(a)** Map: no final city; the route circles and towns become revisitable once enough time has passed · **(b)** Map: no final city; you must return to where you started, and the return leg is harder · **(c)** Map: the route circles, and each full circuit raises every town's goal · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q0871** `[root]` — **Map probability rings** — rule, S2, from gam draft.txt:51. Better cards toward the middle · **(a)** The map is a square of probability rings, with higher-rank nodes likelier toward the centre · **(b)** The map is a square of probability rings, and travelling inward costs more fame per step · **(c)** The map is a square of probability rings, and the centre is only reachable once per run · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q0872** `[root]` — **Route-as-song bonus** — rule, S2, from DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §A12 Australia & Oceania. Repeat the route, strengthen the act · **(a)** Repeating a route pattern you have walked before grants a strengthening bonus · **(b)** Repeating a route pattern grants a bonus that grows with how long the repeated stretch is · **(c)** Repeating a route pattern grants a bonus, and repeating it in reverse grants a different one · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q0873** `[root]` — **The International Final** — rule, S2, from DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §F3 Music, Concerts & Festivals. The regions you toured vote · **(a)** Suits favoured by regions you toured generously score extra in the final show · **(b)** Suits favoured by regions you toured generously score extra, and regions you skipped penalise theirs · **(c)** Each region you toured contributes one favoured suit to a final pool, and lines using them score extra · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0874** `[root]` — **The Pitch Lottery** — rule, S2, from DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §F3 Music, Concerts & Festivals. Bid for first pick · **(a)** Bid gold for first pick of the next three map nodes · **(b)** Bid gold for first pick of the next three nodes; the losing bid is refunded · **(c)** Bid gold for first pick, and the amount bid also determines how much of each node you can see · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0875** `[root]` — **The Route Book** — rule, S2, from DESIGN_RECOMMENDATIONS.md §8 Map-layer systems. Plan the route, then stick to it · **(a)** Plan an intended route before the run; deviating from it costs fame · **(b)** Plan an intended route before the run; following it pays a bonus, and deviating costs nothing · **(c)** Plan an intended route; deviating costs fame, but you may re-plan once per biome · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0876** `[root]` — **Triangle map boss phase** — rule, S2, from DESIGN_DOC.md §15 Map v2 (Triangle). Boss phases in the terrain · **(a)** The map places a boss phase every few tiers, drawn as terrain · **(b)** The map places a boss phase every few tiers, and fights happen only at those phases · **(c)** The map places boss phases every few tiers, and you may pay fame to skip one per run · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q0877** `[root]` — **Triangle map choice** — rule, S2, from DESIGN_DOC.md §15 Map v2 (Triangle). A triangle you narrow as you climb · **(a)** Choose from the next three options; moving left permanently loses the right lanes, and the middle loses both · **(b)** Choose from the next three options; the lanes you lose become visible so you know what you gave up · **(c)** Choose from the next three; lanes lost reopen once per run at a fame cost · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)

- **Q0880** `[root]` — **Special map squares** — rule, S3, from DESIGN_DOC.md §15 Map v1 (Minesweeper). Squares that mean something fixed · **(a)** Map squares carry fixed effects by rank, with higher ranks more likely toward the centre · **(b)** Map squares carry fixed effects by rank, and one rank is designated the shop rank each run · **(c)** Map squares carry fixed effects by rank, and an effect can change a square's rank to change what it is · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0881** `[root]` — **The Egress (event)** — rule, S3, from DESIGN_RECOMMENDATIONS.md §9 Events. - **Q0882** `[root]` — **The Ghost Train** — rule, S3, from DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §E The Fairground, Midway & Business. - **Q0883** `[root]` — **The Locked Room** — rule, S3, from DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §G The Modern Era. - **Q0884** `[root]` — **The Unsanctioned Show** — rule, S3, from DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §B1 Aerial & Wire. Perform where you should not · **(a)** Map node: performing at a forbidden node pays enormous fame, and failing loses the run's tips · **(b)** Map node: performing at a forbidden node pays enormous fame, and failing bars you from the region · **(c)** Map node: a forbidden node pays enormous fame, and merely visiting it marks you for a later boss · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)

- **Q0891** `[root]` — **Improving town (Chautauqua)** — rule, S4, from DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §A10 The Americas. - **Q0892** `[root]` — **Jaded city (Richiardi)** — rule, S4, from DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §C1 Golden-Age Stage Magic. - **Q0893** `[root]` — **Outlawed** — type, S4, from CARD_CATALOG. - **Q0895** `[root]` — **Resort towns** — rule, S4, from DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §E The Fairground, Midway & Business. - **Q0898** `[root]` — **The Scam Booking** — rule, S4, from DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §F3 Music, Concerts & Festivals. This card counts as every suit for flush evaluation (but not for suit-stacking rules), and its prop identity is randomly one of the game's props each time · **(a)** Counts as every suit for flush evaluation but not for stacking, and its prop is a random one of the game's props each time · **(b)** Counts as every suit for both flushes and stacking, and fires no prop of its own · **(c)** Counts as every suit for flushes, and fires the prop of whichever suit is most common on its grid · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)

### V5 - Non-transitive comparison

- **Q1041** `[root]` — **Rock Paper Scissors** — suit, V5, from curated effects pre grid.csv:33. Rock, paper, scissors · **(a)** At game start becomes rock, paper or scissors; stacking on another such card requires winning the throw, with ties broken by rank · **(b)** At game start becomes rock, paper or scissors; it beats one suit and loses to another, and ties never stack · **(c)** It re-rolls its throw every time it is placed, so its stacking legality changes each time · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)

### V6 - Board-derived identity

- **Q1042** `[root]` — **Clever Hans** — skill, V6, from CARD_CATALOG. Counts as a copy of its highest orthogonally-adjacent neighbor's rank; with no neighbors, its rank is 1 · **(a)** This card's rank equals its highest orthogonally adjacent neighbour's rank, or 1 with no neighbours · **(b)** This card's rank equals the sum of its orthogonally adjacent neighbours' ranks, capped at 13 · **(c)** This card's rank and suit both copy whichever neighbour was placed most recently · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q1043** `[root]` — **Hanafuda Reskin** — skill, V6, from CARD_CATALOG. When a town outlaws a suit, all your cards of that suit count as the suit of the adjacent card for that show · **(a)** When a town outlaws a suit, your cards of that suit count as their adjacent card's suit for the show · **(b)** When a town outlaws a suit, your cards of that suit count as a suit of your choice for the show · **(c)** Your cards always count as the suit of whichever orthogonal neighbour was placed most recently · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q1044** `[root]` — **The Turk** — skill, V6, from CARD_CATALOG. Borrows the rank underneath · **(a)** This card's rank is the rank of the card directly beneath it in its stack, or 0 with nothing beneath · **(b)** This card's rank is the summed rank of everything beneath it in its stack, capped at 13 · **(c)** This card's rank is the rank of the card beneath it, and it also inherits that card's suit · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1045** `[root]` — **The Wildcard** — skill, V6, from CARD_CATALOG. Becomes whatever its row already has · **(a)** On every board change, becomes a copy of a rank and suit already present in its row · **(b)** On every board change, becomes a copy of whichever rank and suit is most common in its row · **(c)** On every board change, becomes whichever rank and suit would score its row highest · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1046** `[root]` — **Wild pip** — rank, V6, from DESIGN_DOC.md §6 Card Anatomy & Pips. A pip that borrows from the board · **(a)** Wild pip: takes a rank and suit at random from those present on its row, updating on every board change · **(b)** Wild pip: takes a rank and suit at random from those present on its grid, fixed once it is placed · **(c)** Wild pip: takes whichever rank and suit appear most in its line, updating on every board change · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)

- **Q1047** `[root]` — **The Abacus** — skill, V7, from generated. Adds its neighbours together · **(a)** This card's rank is the sum of its orthogonal neighbours' ranks, capped at the deck's highest rank · **(b)** This card's rank is the sum of its orthogonal neighbours' ranks, uncapped · **(c)** This card's rank is the sum of its neighbours' ranks minus its own printed rank · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q1048** `[root]` — **The Difference** — skill, V7, from generated. Scores the gap, not the value · **(a)** Scores the difference between its own rank and its highest neighbour's · **(b)** Scores the difference between the highest and lowest ranks in its line · **(c)** Scores the difference between its own rank and its lowest neighbour's, doubled if that difference is exactly one · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1049** `[root]` — **The Doubling Act** — stamp, V7, from generated. Twice the number, once the card · **(a)** This card's rank counts as double its printed value for every check · **(b)** This card's rank counts as double for melds only, and normal for stacking · **(c)** This card's rank doubles each time it scores, until it exceeds the deck's highest rank and wraps to 1 · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1050** `[root]` — **The Halving** — stamp, V7, from generated. ---

## What this document deliberately does not contain

- **No flowcharts.** Charts redraw questions, and a chart of a thousand independent content
  rulings tells nobody anything. There is no review-canvas stage for this round.
- **No history and no reasoning.** Every effect is stated as trigger, action, number. Where an
  idea arrived with circus history, a Balatro joker name or a paragraph of why it would be fun,
  all of that was stripped. Provenance is kept as a source citation and nothing more.
- **No balance numbers you should trust.** The numbers in the options exist to make the three
  versions distinguishable from each other. Rarity, cost and tuning are a later pass, run against
  `Tools/scoring_sim.py`, not decided here.
- **No implementation.** No pseudocode, no class names, no file paths, no hooks named as
  contracts. An approved effect is a decision that it belongs in the game, not a spec.
- **No engine-capability audit.** The gate that normally precedes a first question round asks
  whether the engine already ships what a design proposes. It does not apply to a content
  selection round — nothing here is being built yet. **Each approved effect still needs that gate
  run against it before it is implemented**, and a cluster of approved effects sharing a mechanism
  should get one design pass between them rather than one each.
- **No family N.** View, camera and UI effects were excluded by your ruling. The taxonomy still
  carries the ten classes so the hole stays visible, and they can be a later round on their own.

## What happens when the round ends

Every answer becomes one row of a single CSV: the effect, the approved variant in full, its slot,
its class, its rarity band, its provenance, and its status. Rejections are recorded as rejections
so the same idea does not get re-mined out of the same documents next time. Free-text answers are
carried verbatim.
